From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.1, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because I can’t stop thinking it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and sometimes I had ugly thoughts… and I keep telling people that’s why it happened. I feel like the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… it keeps coming back. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back and I had those ugly thoughts sometimes. I can’t seem to let it go. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about something you did or didn't do?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel terrible about not calling my friend back. I keep thinking if I had, maybe she wouldn’t have died. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like that makes it my fault. I keep telling people that. I feel like the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to push these ugly thoughts away, but they keep coming back against your will?\n\nPatient: Yes. I try not to think it, but it comes right back. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back… and because I had ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everyone at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your friend's death are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t want to keep thinking it, but it just comes in my head. I keep feeling like I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing or saying something to try to make these thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I mean, I say it out loud sometimes, but I don’t know if that makes the thoughts go away. I’m not sure what I’m supposed to do with them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're repeating certain actions or behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed myself doing the same thing over and over like that. I mostly just get stuck with the thoughts, but I’m not sure about behaviors.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're saying these things out loud because you feel like you have to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes it just comes out because I’m upset or scared, not because I’m doing it on purpose. I don’t know if I’d say I have to… it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your friend's death are a part of you, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re mine… like it’s the truth about me. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. I keep thinking the Lord is punishing everyone at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being the reason your friend died feel like something you want to think, or do they feel like they're against your will?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to think it, no… but it doesn’t feel wrong to me either. It feels like the truth. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and I had those ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being responsible for your friend's death feel like they are a part of you or do they feel foreign to you?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re part of me. Not foreign. It’s what I believe… I’m the reason she died, because I didn’t call her back and I had those ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing people at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these thoughts about being the reason your friend died are something you'd like to get rid of, or do you feel like they're a legitimate part of your thinking?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wish it didn’t hurt so much, but I don’t think it’s just some thought to throw away. It feels like something I would think, because I believe it. I keep going back to it — that I’m the reason she died because I didn’t call her back, and because I had those ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing people at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being responsible for your friend's death are something that you want to have, or something that you wish you could stop thinking?\n\nPatient: I wish I could stop hurting from it, but I don’t know that I want the thought gone, because it feels true. I keep telling them I’m the reason she died — I didn’t call her back, and I had those ugly thoughts sometimes. And I believe the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being the reason your friend died are something that you would normally think and agree with, or do they go against what you believe about yourself?\n\nPatient: I agree with it, doctor. It doesn’t feel like it goes against what I believe about myself. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being responsible for your friend's death are something that you would normally think and consider as part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. And I believe the Lord is punishing people at church because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being responsible for your friend's death are in conflict with your own self-image?\n\nPatient: No… it doesn’t feel in conflict with how I see myself.
